This is the living status document for the project. Its purpose is to make the real project state visible without relying on memory, assumptions, or Git history alone. Every important change should update this file with what was done, what was tested, what remains unfinished, and what must not be started without prerequisites.

The product split into `FULL` and `ATTENDANCE` editions is tracked in the dedicated [Attendance Edition Split Checklist](ATTENDANCE_EDITION_CHECKLIST.md). That document is authoritative for edition scope, implementation order, acceptance gates, and release readiness.

Audit scope on 2026-05-25: backend API routes, Prisma schema models, frontend routes/pages, frontend API wrappers, K40 device code path, and safe verification commands.

## Status Legend

| Marker | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `[x]` | Implemented and verified by at least a basic code or command check |
| `[~]` | Implemented in code, but still needs complete validation, tests, or hardening |
| `[ ]` | Not implemented |
| `[!]` | Known risk, failing check, or partially wired feature |
| `[BLOCKED]` | Not safe to implement until prerequisites are available |

## Maintenance Rules

- No important work should be considered complete unless this file is updated.
- Any work that affects the `FULL` / `ATTENDANCE` split must also update `ATTENDANCE_EDITION_CHECKLIST.md`.
- Keep a clear distinction between "implemented in code" and "verified by test".
- Any device-related change must pass the relevant automated Device/K40 regression suites. The real-hardware K40 checklist is mandatory only when the owning work package requires hardware—specifically Attendance Phase 2.7 and the later Release Gate—and otherwise must be recorded as `Not run`.
- Do not start support for a new device model without the device, a real device log sample, or temporary test access.
- If a test was not run, write `Not run`; do not leave the result ambiguous.
- Any change involving money, attendance, memberships, door access, devices, or reports must have a regression test plan.
- If a checklist item is marked `[x]`, record the command, manual test, or file evidence that supports it.

## Frontend Loading Overlay Rollout Plan

Current UI follow-up: `UI-1.7` is complete per the canonical UI plan, including
page-by-page FULL parity, Dashboard detail tables/tooltips, Employee detail/edit
and enrollment presentation, custom selects, table typography/Light contrast and
Attendance header cleanup. Physical enrollment/pilot and production Installer
acceptance are separate and remain open.

Audit date: 2026-05-27.

Existing reusable overlay: `client/src/components/AppLoadingOverlay.tsx`.
Existing overlay styles and keyframes: `client/src/index.css`.

Decision rules:

- Use the full-screen app loading overlay only for app bootstrap, route-level first loads where the page is not meaningful until core data arrives, and heavy report regeneration.
- Keep table/card skeletons for lists and dashboards so users can see the page structure while data refreshes.
- Keep inline spinners or disabled labels for submits, exports, member search modals, device commands, fingerprint enrollment, and other user-triggered actions.
- The overlay must always unblock on API failure and show an error state elsewhere; it must not hide recoverable page content indefinitely.

### Rollout Stages

| Stage | Scope | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. Loading audit and rules | Review frontend routes and classify loading behavior | `[x]` | 22 content routes reviewed; 9 existing report surfaces already use `AppLoadingOverlay` |
| 2. Global bootstrap overlay | Add one app-shell loading coordinator around `App` / `DashboardLayout` | `[x]` | `GlobalLoadingProvider` is mounted in `App`; report-card route transitions now preload the overlay before navigation, and app-wide automatic triggering is deferred until a non-report slow route is observed |
| 3. Standardize report overlays | Normalize existing report overlay usage | `[x]` | Payroll report tab, membership report tab, attendance, customer activity, debt aging, renewal forecast, payroll variance, attendance compliance, and cashflow now use `useGlobalLoading` instead of rendering `AppLoadingOverlay` directly |
| 4. Add blocking route overlays | Add overlay to routes that cannot render useful content before initial data | `[x]` | Member detail, member edit, and sound settings initial loads now use the global loading hook; action loading remains local |
| 5. Preserve local loading patterns | Keep skeletons and inline spinners where interaction should remain visible | `[x]` | Client-side navigation QA confirmed dashboard, attendance tables, entry logs, member lists, device tools, expenses, membership plans, and staff groups stay on local loading patterns; membership plans loading was upgraded from text to skeleton cards |
| 6. Verification pass | Visual and failure-state QA | `[x]` | Automated Chrome/CDP QA covered member detail/edit, sound settings, report route transitions, attendance/cashflow reports, reduced motion, scroll lock, RTL/LTR font checks, and local-loading routes. Attendance was verified through direct and client-side navigation; the artificial Fetch-pause method can leave Chrome localhost requests pending and is not treated as an app failure |

### Route Loading Treatment

| Route / surface | Current evidence | Recommended treatment |
| --- | --- | --- |
| App shell | `GlobalLoadingProvider` is mounted in `App.tsx`; report-card transitions start a temporary global loading entry before navigation | Add app-wide route-transition triggering only if a non-report slow route is observed |
| `/dashboard` | React Query summary loading, live event loading, details modal loading | Keep section/modal loading only; global overlay only during app boot |
| `/attendance` | Daily attendance table/card skeletons and drawer skeletons | Keep local skeletons; no route overlay |
| `/entry-logs` | Entry-log table skeletons and manual-entry submit state | Keep local skeletons/spinners; no route overlay |
| `/devices` | Device card skeletons, scan/test/diagnose/enroll command states | Keep local skeletons and command-level spinners; no route overlay |
| `/members` | `MembersTable` skeleton rows | Keep table skeletons; no route overlay |
| `/members/add` | `MemberWizard` loads plans, groups, shifts, and devices internally | Keep wizard-level disabled/loading labels and submit spinner; no route overlay |
| `/members/:id` | Blocking profile load now registers with global loading while no profile exists | Keep delete/payment action loading local |
| `/members/:id/edit` | Blocking member + membership load now registers with global loading before wizard defaults exist | Keep save action loading local |
| `/payments/memberships` | Member search modal loading, profile loading, submit spinner | Keep modal/section/action loading; no route overlay |
| `/payments/payroll` | Member search, summary/outstanding/history loading, submit spinner | Keep modal/section/action loading; no route overlay |
| `/payments/expenses` | Expense table loading and save/reverse states | Keep table/action loading; no route overlay |
| `/reports` dashboard | React Query dashboard summary loading | Keep dashboard cards local; no overlay for the report index cards |
| `/reports` payroll tab | Uses `useGlobalLoading` for report loading | Keep table and member-search loading local |
| `/reports` membership tab | Uses `useGlobalLoading` for report loading | Keep filter option loading and export state local |
| `/reports/attendance` | Uses `useGlobalLoading` for page/report load | Keep KPI/table/drawer skeletons local |
| `/reports/customer-activity` | Uses `useGlobalLoading` only while no generated summary exists | Keep tab loading local |
| `/reports/debt-aging` | Uses `useGlobalLoading` for report loading | Keep filter option loading and export state local |
| `/reports/renewal-forecast` | Uses `useGlobalLoading` for report loading | Keep filter option loading and export state local |
| `/reports/payroll-variance` | Uses `useGlobalLoading` for report loading | Keep filter option loading and export state local |
| `/reports/attendance-compliance` | Uses `useGlobalLoading` for report loading | Drawer loading remains local |
| `/reports/cashflow` | Uses `useGlobalLoading` for report loading | Keep export state local |
| `/settings/membership-plans` | List loading now uses small skeleton cards | Keep local loading; no full overlay |
| `/settings/audit-logs` | Uses table skeleton rows and a local detail-modal loading state | Keep loading local so filters and surrounding Settings navigation remain usable |
| `/settings/staff-groups` | Skeleton cards already exist | Keep skeleton cards; no full overlay |
| `/settings/sounds` | Blocking assets/settings load now registers with global loading before the draft exists | Keep save/upload/playback states local |

## Audit Log Coverage Matrix

Audit date: 2026-07-19; last implementation update: 2026-08-01. This matrix was reconciled from production `AuditLog` writer calls, mutation/export routers, the current database, and the protected viewer. It is the source of truth for future AuditLog work; a row must not be marked complete merely because the business table itself keeps a ledger.

Status rules for this matrix:

- `[x]`: the production writer exists, required attribution/data is present, and focused verification covers the contract.
- `[~]`: writer code exists, but atomicity, failure behavior, attribution, field completeness, or focused tests still need hardening.
- `[ ]`: the business operation exists but does not currently write the required global `AuditLog` row.
- `[N/A]`: intentionally excluded from global AuditLog to avoid duplicating operational/read-only noise.

| Area | Production action codes | Status | Verified evidence / remaining hardening |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

### Intentionally Excluded From Global AuditLog

| Operation | Status | Reason / existing source of truth |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Opening pages, dashboard refresh, search, filter, sort and pagination | `[N/A]` | Read-only UI noise; it would hide meaningful security/business mutations. |
| Normal report viewing without export | `[N/A]` | Read-only access; export/download is audited separately because it releases bulk personal/financial data. |
| Inbox read/unread actions | `[N/A]` | Per-user UI state, not a sensitive business mutation. |
| Local language/theme switching | `[N/A]` | Browser-only preference; audit only if future backend-persisted administrative settings are added. |
| Normal K40 polling and automatic device scans | `[N/A]` | Operational state belongs in `DeviceRawLog`, `AttendanceLog`, `AccessViolation`, device status fields and backend logs. |
| Automatic authorized/denied attendance events | `[N/A]` | `AttendanceLog`, `DeviceRawLog` and `AccessViolation` are the high-volume evidence ledgers; global AuditLog is reserved for administrative/manual actions. |

### Audit Writer Completion Gate

Every required writer row must meet all conditions before changing `[ ]`/`[~]` to `[x]`:

1. Capture actor ID/type, recorded role, session ID, IP, user-agent, action, target type/ID, result and timestamp.
2. Store safe before/after summaries and useful metadata; never store passwords, tokens, credentials, biometric/fingerprint templates or full secret-bearing payloads.
3. Write a successful audit in the same database transaction as the business mutation whenever both are database operations.
4. Roll back the business mutation if its required AuditLog insert fails; record meaningful failed sensitive attempts separately after rollback where appropriate.
5. Verify 401/403 produce no business write, the permitted path produces exactly the expected audit, duplicate/idempotent behavior is explicit, and test fixtures clean up to zero.
6. Add English/Dari/Pashto action/target labels and verify the viewer shows readable text while retaining the raw action code.
7. Add the focused test command and result to this checklist, then perform one real browser operation and reconcile the AuditLog target against its business record with a read-only database query.

Implementation order from this matrix: finish the remaining sensitive device time/unlock/log-clear commands next, followed by sound actions, report exports, and finally future schema-only workflows.

### Design Rules

- Keep semantic `<table>` markup through the shared component; do not replace tables with div grids for real tabular data.
- Use `default` for operational tables, `compact` for modal/dashboard tables, and `report` for report-heavy pages.
- Do not force every table to the exact same density; visual language should be shared, but reporting pages may stay denser.
- Do not delete legacy table CSS until all references are migrated and checked with `rg`.
- Keep existing pagination, export, row click, drawer, and action behavior unchanged during style migration.
- Any changed table route must be checked in both light and dark themes because previous bugs appeared during theme switching and hover exit.

## K40 Device Support

Real setup document: [`docs/k40-setup.md`](../operations/k40-setup.md).

### Implemented Capabilities

- `[x]` Network connection using configured IP and port.
- `[x]` Default ZKTeco port `4370` support through configuration.
- `[x]` Device connection test.
- `[x]` Device diagnostics and subnet scan helpers.
- `[x]` Device diagnostics detect Windows local IP state, duplicate PC IP, device/PC IP conflicts, link-local addresses, subnet mismatch, and unreachable TCP port.
- `[x]` Device diagnostics show a simplified operator summary first, with raw NIC/recommendation details moved into an advanced section.
- `[x]` Device user listing.
- `[x]` Create or update a user on the device.
- `[x]` Delete a user from the device.
- `[x]` Start fingerprint enrollment.
- `[x]` Read device time.
- `[x]` Set device time.
- `[x]` Read attendance logs from the device.
- `[x]` Periodic polling for new logs.
- `[x]` Auto/push mode switching support in device manager configuration.
- `[x]` Duplicate attendance protection using a generated dedupe key.
- `[x]` Raw device log persistence before attendance processing, including processing status for processed, duplicate, invalid, and failed events.
- `[x]` Protected read-only raw-log viewer in Devices with status/date/search filters, pagination, support details, collapsible technical payload, and device diagnostics permission gating.
- `[x]` Door unlock through the device command or relay fallback.
- `[x]` Access violation logging.
- `[x]` Pending membership activation on first authorized entry.
- `[x]` Live device event publishing and inbox/device feed integration.

### Known K40 Risks

- `[!]` Unstable network can make polling fail and temporarily mark the device as `ERROR`.
- `[!]` If device time and server time drift, attendance reports can become inaccurate.
- `[!]` Large attendance log volume on the device can make reads slow.
- `[!]` Device user IDs must be numeric for the current processing path.
- `[!]` Device user IDs are currently globally unique in the database; this can become a problem with multiple devices using overlapping IDs.
- `[!]` `USB` and `SERIAL` connection types exist in schema/UI, but the real manager only creates clients for `NETWORK` and `ZKTECO_PULL_SDK`.
- `[!]` Clearing device logs must remain manual and cautious.
- `[x]` Raw device logs are stored, viewable in-app, and downloadable as a sanitized support bundle for processing/audit diagnosis; realistic database-backed processing and cleanup are covered by the isolated local-PostgreSQL integration suite.

### End-to-End Membership-to-K40 Workflow Test Plan

This plan and its completed result log are historical `FULL` access-control evidence. The separate `ATTENDANCE` TIME_CLOCK matrix was completed under Phase 2.7 on 2026-08-23 (see k40-setup and the Attendance checklist). It is not the later O.2.6 Inbox hardware acceptance, which remains open. Device changes and Release still require their owning hardware reruns.

Goal: verify the real business path from member setup and membership/shift rules through the K40 device, attendance logs, dashboard refresh, inbox alerts, and member detail tabs.

Prerequisites before starting:

| Check | Owner | Status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| App is running and the tester is logged in as a `GYM_OWNER` | User | `[x]` | Confirmed during the 2026-06-08 real-device workflow session with access to members, devices, attendance, entry logs, dashboard, and inbox |
| K40 is enabled and connected in `/devices` | User | `[x]` | Confirmed from Device tools before the real-device test batch |
| K40 device time is correct or synced from Device tools | User | `[x]` | Confirmed synced with the app before the real-device test batch |
| Baseline dashboard and entry-log counts are noted | User | `[x]` | User confirmed dashboard and entry-log reconciliation during the completed 2026-06-08 real-device workflow |
| One active customer with a valid active membership is available on K40 | User | `[x]` | Confirmed by successful fingerprint access test |
| One inactive/expired/debtor customer is available on K40 | User | `[x]` | Confirmed by successful denied-access fingerprint test |
| One staff member with a staff group, default shift, and K40 fingerprint is available | User | `[x]` | Confirmed by successful staff fingerprint check-in test with ali ahmadi |

Manual test cases:

| # | Scenario | User action | Expected result | Owner |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Active customer allowed access | Scan the active customer's fingerprint on K40 | Door access is allowed; `/entry-logs` shows one authorized `IN/Entry`; dashboard Live Events and Today's entries refresh; no denial inbox item is created | User verifies in browser; Codex records result |
| 2 | Duplicate ingest protection | Fetch/read the same K40 logs again from Device tools, or wait for another poll of the same device rows | The same K40 log does not create a second Entry Logs row; totals do not double-count | User verifies; Codex records result |
| 3 | Expired/inactive/debtor customer denied | Scan the denied-test customer's fingerprint on K40 | Door access is denied; `/entry-logs` shows `DENIED` with the correct reason; dashboard denied count/reason updates; inbox warning is created | User verifies; Codex records result |
| 4 | Staff allowed check-in | During the staff member's allowed shift window, scan the staff fingerprint | `/entry-logs` shows authorized `IN`; `/attendance` shows the staff member inside/late/on-time based on shift rules; dashboard Inside Now updates | User verifies; Codex records result |
| 5 | Staff checkout and overtime | Scan the same staff fingerprint after an open staff session, including after shift end if possible | The event is accepted as `OUT`, not denied as a new entry; attendance closes the session; overtime is calculated when applicable | User verifies; Codex records result |
| 6 | New out-of-shift entry denied | Use a staff/customer user with no open session outside the allowed access window | The event is denied with `Entry is not allowed at this time` or the localized equivalent; no attendance session is opened | User verifies; Codex records result |
| 7 | Staff early access window | Configure a staff shift with `Early access` set to 15 minutes, then scan once inside that pre-shift window and once before that window | The scan inside the early window is allowed as `IN`; the scan before the window is denied; attendance can show early clock-in, but payroll/overtime still follows shift start/end rules | User verifies; Codex records result |
| 8 | Dashboard reconciliation | After tests 1-7, open `/dashboard` and compare counters with `/entry-logs` and `/attendance` | Today's entries, denied attempts, inside count, live events, weekly chart, and denied-reason chart match the tested events | User checks; Codex records result |
| 9 | Member detail reconciliation | Open each tested member detail page and inspect Overview, Attendance, Access & devices, and Membership/Shifts & salary tabs | Member detail shows the expected payment/membership/attendance/access violation/device history in both light and dark themes | User checks; Codex records result |
| 10 | Inbox and device feed | Open Inbox tabs after allowed, denied, and disconnect/reconnect events | Denied access and disconnect alerts appear once; read/unread behavior works; device feed entries match the tested events | User checks; Codex records result |

## Non-K40 Devices

Current decision: do not implement support for other devices yet.

Reasons:

- No other physical device is available for real testing.
- Face recognition device log formats can differ from K40 attendance logs.
- The current iClock route receives and logs requests, but does not yet process them into attendance events.
- The current `zklib-js` path is appropriate for K40-style ZKTeco polling, not proven complete face-device support.
- Starting without hardware can create half-finished code and risk the working K40 path.

Prerequisites before starting support for a new device:

- `[ ]` The physical device is available, or
- `[ ]` A real request/log sample from the device is available, or
- `[ ]` Temporary network access to the device is available for testing, and
- `[ ]` Exact model, firmware, port, communication mode, and sample logs are recorded.

- `[x]` Extended the real-service suite to 9/9 for physical missing-dump persistence. A valid manifest/log bundle with no `database.dump`, an existing COMPLETED/NOT_VERIFIED Job and a RUNNING/verifying SAFE_BACKUP prove exact 409, COMPLETED/FAILED verification with null `verifiedAt` and preserved artifact metadata, FAILED/100 run before Audit, exact failure attribution, byte-identical retained manifest/log, empty uploads and no stranded state.

Do not repeat successful real restore/browser drills.

## Report Export Automation Plan

Goal: stop relying on one-by-one manual export checks for every report. The automated suite should know which reports export which file types, download or generate each supported file, and verify that the output is valid in English, Dari, and Pashto.

### Export Matrix

| Surface | Route / endpoint | CSV | XLSX | PDF | Export owner | Automation status | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

| Attendance report | `/api/v1/reports/attendance/export.xlsx`, `/export.pdf`; CSV built in `AttendanceReportPage` | Client | Server | Server | Mixed | `[x]` | Backend XLSX fixture test parses Summary/Daily/Members/Evidence and reconciles localized headers, member/event/source/result labels, dates, names, devices, and calculated values in English/Dari/Pashto. PDF.js extracts each data-bearing PDF and validates localized KPI words, date, member name, and numeric content. Playwright verifies daily/members/evidence CSV downloads in all three languages |
| Customer activity report | `/api/v1/reports/customer-activity/export.csv`, `/export.xlsx` | Server | Server | - | Backend | `[x]` | Data-bearing automation validates CSV rows and parsed XLSX Summary/table sheets for visitedToday, inactiveMembers, and lowEngagement in English/Dari/Pashto, including localized headers/state labels, visit counts/timestamps, 7/30-day KPIs, member/plan fields, and never-visited handling |
| Debt aging report | `/api/v1/reports/debt-aging/export.csv`, `/export.xlsx` | Server | Server | - | Backend | `[x]` | Data-bearing automation reconciles 30/31/61/91-day bucket boundaries, settled exclusion, 4000 outstanding, 4 debtors, 1000 average, 2000 largest debt, 46.67% collection rate, localized member/status/bucket/date-field text, and parsed XLSX Summary/Buckets/Debtors content in English/Dari/Pashto |
| Renewal forecast report | `/api/v1/reports/renewal-forecast/export.csv`, `/export.xlsx`, `/export.pdf` | Server | Server | Server | Backend | `[x]` | Data-bearing automation reconciles CSV and parsed XLSX Summary/By Plan/Forecast content in English/Dari/Pashto: two in-horizon rows, one excluded out-of-range row, 2300 revenue risk, 500 debt risk, 50% historical renewal rate, and one expected renewal. PDF.js extracts each PDF and validates localized KPI words and included member/plan text while confirming the out-of-range member is absent |

| Cashflow report | `/api/v1/reports/cashflow/export.csv`, `/export.xlsx` | Server | Server | - | Backend | `[x]` | Service-level CSV/XLSX content coverage exists, browser-downloaded files were manually inspected, and backend export health runner covers English/Dari/Pashto structure |

Language-aware backend export contract | `[x]` | Add `lang=en|fa|ps` handling to backend exports | Server-owned export schemas accept `lang`; shared export labels drive CSV headers, XLSX sheet names, status/type labels, and PDF static text; `npm.cmd run test:reports-exports` passed on 2026-07-13 across English, Dari, and Pashto no-data exports |

Frontend download automation | `[x]` | Client-generated CSV exports for Attendance Report, Daily Attendance, and Entry Logs | `npm.cmd run test:report-downloads` switches language, clicks export, captures and parses each CSV, then reconciles UTF-8 BOM, localized headers/enums, stable identity/device/note/timestamp fields, report dates/counts, row shape, and no mojibake |

### Current Constraints

- Server-owned exports have a shared English/Dari/Pashto label contract. All backend CSV/XLSX report surfaces have data-bearing reconciliation, and Attendance/Renewal PDFs now have automated extracted-text reconciliation in all three languages.
- Browser-level automation is required for client-generated CSV files because they are assembled in React, not by the API. The focused Playwright suite now parses and reconciles data rows for Daily Attendance, Entry Logs, and Attendance Report daily/members/evidence CSVs in all three languages.
- PDF text validation uses PDF.js in Node. Because PDF extraction can return right-to-left words in visual order, localized assertions reconcile the expected words without depending on RTL word order; browser QA remains the visual-rendering check.

- `[x]` Frontend lint passes with 0 errors and 0 warnings; the former 59-warning backlog was resolved on 2026-07-16 without disabling rules.
- `[x]` Device processing has baseline unit-style coverage plus isolated local-PostgreSQL integration tests using realistic K40-shaped payloads; authorized, duplicate, invalid, and denied persistence paths pass with verified cleanup.
- `[x]` `DeviceRawLog` inspection and filtered support-bundle export UI/API are implemented, and database-backed processing integration coverage now verifies the stored status/attendance links.
- `[!]` `USB` and `SERIAL` device types appear in schema/UI but are not supported by the actual device manager client creation path.
- `[!]` iClock endpoints are logging/test endpoints, not a complete attendance ingestion path.

- `[!]` License management, report-job workflow, backend system settings, translation management, and theme persistence exist in schema or planning only and should not be treated as implemented features. Auth, admin-user management, audit writers, and backup/recovery have real API/UI surfaces but still need deeper role/action QA and hardening.
- `[~]` Core membership, expense, Cashflow, and the primary monthly Payroll path now have realistic reconciliation coverage. Remaining financial hardening includes `PER_SHIFT`/`PER_HOUR` salary calculation, percentage overtime, fixed deduction modes, concurrent first-payment creation, and broader UI automation.

## Work Classification After Audit

| Category | Items | Practical meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Done and basically verified | Backend TypeScript check, frontend TypeScript check, health route, backend route inventory, frontend route inventory, K40 event processing | These are safe baseline facts, but they do not prove full business correctness. K40 processing has both recorded real-device regression results and isolated database coverage |
| Implemented but not fully verified | Members, memberships, payments, expenses, payroll, attendance, reports, inbox, sounds, dashboard, K40 management, end-to-end AuditLog writer coverage | These are the main working areas; each needs targeted tests or manual validation. The Audit Log viewer itself is verified, but not every sensitive business mutation writes an audit row yet |
| Implemented with known risk | iClock raw endpoints, USB/SERIAL options in UI/schema | These should be stabilized before adding new large features |
| Not implemented beyond schema or not wired | License management, report jobs, backend system settings, backend translations, backend theme persistence | Do not present these as finished features |
| Blocked for now | Face devices, other brands, face enrollment, production USB/SERIAL support | Do not start without hardware, real logs, or temporary access |

## Recommended Work Order

1. Protect the baseline.
   Record this checklist in Git before starting application code changes, keep the current passing backend/frontend TypeScript checks as the baseline, and define the database backup procedure.

2. Protect the clean frontend lint baseline.
   The lint gate now passes with 0 errors and 0 warnings; new warnings should be fixed before merging future changes.

3. Stabilize the K40 path with the real device.
   Run the manual K40 regression checklist and record every result. This is the highest business-risk area because it affects attendance, access decisions, and door opening.

4. Add automated tests around device decisions.
   Cover duplicate prevention, authorized access, debtor access denial, expired membership denial, inactive/blacklisted members, out-of-shift access, and pending membership activation on first entry.

5. Validate money-related flows.
   Test membership creation, renewal, cancellation, payments, debt, expenses, reversals, payroll payments, and cashflow totals with realistic data.

6. Validate attendance and reports.
   Run existing report tests, add missing report/export tests, and manually compare attendance, compliance, debt, renewal, payroll variance, customer activity, and cashflow outputs against known data.

7. Harden frontend workflows.
   Walk through member creation/editing, membership payment, payroll payment, expenses, reports, inbox, sounds, date filters, and device pages. Record any UI issues as separate checklist items.

8. Follow accepted Phase boundaries for remaining work.
   Licensing is now planned explicitly for Phase 6.2–6.6. Report jobs, backend settings persistence, translation/theme persistence, face devices, other brands, and USB/SERIAL production support remain deferred until separately scoped. The Audit Log viewer is no longer schema-only; remaining AuditLog work is writer coverage for sensitive workflows. Auth/admin and backup/recovery have real surfaces now, but still need deeper role/action QA and hardening.

### Smart Backup & Recovery Assistant

Goal: make backup, export/import, and recovery safe enough for installed/offline customers, while simple enough for a remote customer to follow without understanding PostgreSQL dumps, manifests, hashes, scripts, or maintenance internals.

Product decision:

- `[x]` Keep the existing backup/restore safety foundation as the trusted engine.
- `[~]` Replace the current operator-facing workflow with an assistant-style experience. The primary UI should show one clear action, current step, progress, result, and next step.
- `[~]` Treat technical details as support/advanced information, not as the main customer workflow.
- `[x]` Keep production restore as a downtime/vendor-guided operation. The app may prepare and gate the restore, but the final restore must remain controlled and auditable.

Primary customer actions:

- `[x]` `Create Safe Backup`: first assistant version runs tool readiness checks through existing page state, creates a backup, waits for job completion, verifies the manifest/hash automatically, refreshes the list, opens the result, and shows `Safe backup ready`. User browser QA passed on 2026-06-23.
- `[x]` `Export / Support Package`: creates a verified ZIP support package with `database.dump`, `manifest.json`, `backup.log`, and a clean `support-summary.json` for USB/HDD transfer or remote support. The staging folder is removed after the ZIP is created so customers have one clear file to send. Secrets, `.env`, database passwords, and raw command lines are excluded. User browser QA passed on 2026-06-26 for ZIP-only output.
- `[x]` `Import Backup`: assistant accepts a backup folder or support-package ZIP, scans folder selections before upload, extracts ZIP packages server-side, verifies SHA-256/size through the existing backend import engine, registers the backup in inventory, opens the imported backup, and shows a simple pass/fail result. Folder browser QA passed for valid and invalid imports on 2026-06-23; ZIP browser QA passed for valid and invalid packages on 2026-06-26.
- `[x]` `Prepare Recovery`: first assistant version is implemented in the selected-backup details panel. It starts the existing protected restore-preparation flow, tracks restore-test and emergency-backup progress, auto-verifies the emergency safety backup before `Ready for confirmation`, creates a controlled handoff after the restore phrase, and guides the vendor to maintenance mode. User browser QA passed on 2026-06-27; maintenance mode was cleared and normal-user access remained OK.

Assistant UI requirements:

- `[x]` Show a single progress panel with approximate percent, current action, elapsed time, and a final success/failure state. `Create Safe Backup`, `Export / Support Package`, `Import Backup`, and `Prepare Recovery` now have this.
- `[x]` Use friendly step labels such as `Checking backup tools`, `Creating backup`, `Verifying backup`, `Preparing recovery`, and `Ready for support`. Create-backup, export/support package, import, and recovery preparation flows are covered.
- `[x]` Hide manifest JSON, raw logs, SHA details, restore handoff paths, and script paths behind `Advanced details`. Backup table no longer exposes SHA, and selected-backup details now keep these values in a collapsed support-only panel.
- `[x]` Show only the next valid action. The table now exposes only the details action, direct `Restore test` is hidden from the customer flow, unverified backups show a single `Verify` next action, verified backups move into the support/recovery assistant cards, and failed backups show a safe not-ready state. User browser QA passed on 2026-06-27.
- `[x]` Remove manual typing of the selected backup folder name from the customer flow. The selected folder is now locked from the restore job; only the final restore phrase remains manual.
- `[x]` Provide clear remote-support instructions and copyable status summaries in English, Dari, and Pashto. Support-package results now show what ZIP to send, and the copy action generates a safe localized summary without secrets, `.env` files, passwords, or raw restore commands.

Safety rules that must stay:

- `[x]` Use a managed backup root outside the project directory. Packaged production default is `C:\ProgramData\EntryControlSystem\backups`; this workstation uses `E:\AtlasGymBackups` until installer migration.
- `[x]` Keep PowerShell scripts as the trusted local backup/restore engines until a safer replacement is proven.
- `[x]` Use explicit tool paths, no user-built shell strings, sanitized logs, and timeouts.
- `[x]` Allow only one running backup/restore-preparation operation at a time.
- `[x]` Require `database.dump`, `manifest.json`, and `backup.log` for a valid backup package.
- `[x]` Reject unsupported dump filenames; restore/import paths only accept `database.dump` inside the selected backup folder.
- `[x]` Verify SHA-256 and size against the manifest before import, restore-test, handoff, or production restore.
- `[x]` Run production restore preparation only for `VENDOR_OWNER` with `RESTORE_DATABASE`.
- `[x]` Run restore-test against a temporary database before any production restore handoff.
- `[x]` Create and auto-verify a fresh emergency backup before final restore handoff. The internal type remains `PRE_MIGRATION`, but the customer-facing UI labels it as an emergency backup.
- `[x]` Keep restore-maintenance mode for normal-route 503 gating during controlled restore work.
- `[x]` Write audit logs for backup, import, support-package export, verify, restore-test, emergency backup, handoff, maintenance start/clear, and failed blocked attempts.
- `[x]` Add production-restore completion and post-restore verification audit paths after final restore execution reporting is wired. The UI surfaces `production-restore-report_*.json` sidecars, runs an in-app post-restore check after restart, writes `post-restore-check_*.json`, and records the restore job completion state when the check passes.

Current implemented foundation:

- `[x]` Protected backup API/UI, Prisma `BackupJob` tracking, manual backup creation, inventory/details, manifest/hash verification, and permission-aware actions.
- `[x]` Verified ZIP-only support package export into the managed backup root for remote support or external drive handoff.
- `[x]` Safe import registration into the managed backup root without restoring production data.
- `[x]` Restore-test helper with Windows PostgreSQL tool auto-detection.
- `[x]` Restore preparation creates a `RestoreJob`, validates the selected backup, runs restore-test, and starts a fresh emergency backup.
- `[x]` Controlled production restore helper script refuses unsafe paths, missing files, invalid hashes, restore-test target databases, and active DB connections by default.
- `[x]` Controlled handoff generation records handoff path/script/command on the restore job.
- `[x]` Restore-maintenance status/start/clear APIs and middleware block normal routes while leaving health/auth and backup controls available.
- `[x]` Settings > Backups displays latest handoff/report sidecar files for support/debugging, and surfaces production-restore report status in the normal selected-backup details panel.

Implementation plan:

- `[x]` Add an `OperationRun` assistant orchestration model and API response shape for long-running backup/recovery flows. Safe backup, import, support package, recovery preparation, restore handoff, maintenance start, and post-restore check now create/update durable operation runs, and the backup settings response exposes active runs for future resume/polling UI.
- `[x]` Implement `Create Safe Backup` as one operation: precheck -> backup -> auto-verify -> summary. The UI orchestrates the visible assistant while the backend now records the durable `SAFE_BACKUP` operation run.
- `[x]` Replace the backup page primary controls with assistant cards and a live progress/timeline panel. Create-backup, export/support package, import, and selected-backup recovery preparation are converted.
- `[x]` Move current technical details into `Advanced details`.
- `[x]` Implement export/support package generation for verified backups. It stages the package files, creates one ZIP archive for transfer, and removes the staging folder.
- `[x]` Upgrade import to accept a full folder/package with friendlier error messages and optional support package metadata. Folder import and ZIP extraction are implemented; folder and ZIP browser QA passed.
- `[x]` Implement `Prepare Recovery` as one operation: verify -> restore-test -> emergency backup -> auto-verify emergency backup -> handoff -> maintenance readiness. The UI-orchestrated assistant is implemented using existing APIs and polling; user browser QA passed on 2026-06-27.
- `[x]` Add post-restore report ingestion and automated API smoke guidance after the app restarts. The selected-backup UI now displays production restore report sidecars, offers a post-restore check action, verifies the running app/current database, writes a post-restore sidecar report, and stores the final restore-job completion summary.
- `[x]` Add scheduled backup setup, retention cleanup, external-drive copy, and safe backup storage setup after the assistant workflow is stable. Backend scheduler/config, schedule run-now, local `backup-config.json`, scheduled-backup retention cleanup, external-drive detection/copy endpoints, automatic/configured backup root validation, frequency-bucket storage, multi-schedule profiles, and Settings > Backups controls are implemented. Browser/filesystem QA for multi-profile scheduling, retention, backup storage, and external-drive copy passed.

Scheduled backup frequency-bucket redesign:

- `[x]` Audit current scheduled-backup behavior before implementation. Findings on 2026-07-05: the UI/API/config support `hourly`, `daily`, `weekly`, `monthly`, and `yearly`; the scheduler uses frequency for timing; `backup-database.ps1` receives `ScheduleFrequency`; new scheduled backups are still stored under `BackupRoot\scheduled\<timestamp>_<frequency>_scheduled`; legacy scheduled backups can also live under `BackupRoot\daily\<timestamp>_scheduled`; backend inventory IDs currently assume only `typeDir/folderName`; retention treats all scheduled backups as one pool and uses `retention.daily` as the only keep count; `scheduleFrequency` is written to the manifest for new scheduled backups but is not yet exposed in `BackupItem` or stored on `BackupJob`.
- `[x]` Define the canonical storage layout before code changes: keep backup type as the top-level folder and store scheduled policy buckets under it, e.g. `BackupRoot\scheduled\hourly\...`, `BackupRoot\scheduled\daily\...`, `BackupRoot\scheduled\weekly\...`, `BackupRoot\scheduled\monthly\...`, and `BackupRoot\scheduled\yearly\...`. Keep `manual`, `pre-migration`, and `support-packages` as separate top-level folders.
- `[x]` Keep backward compatibility for existing backups. The scanner, details API, verify flow, support package export, restore-test flow, recovery preparation, handoff generation, and post-restore check must continue to accept old `BackupRoot\daily\<folder>` scheduled backups and current transitional `BackupRoot\scheduled\<timestamp>_<frequency>_scheduled` backups.
- `[x]` Promote scheduled frequency to a first-class field. `scheduleFrequency` is parsed from manifest/path, added to `BackupFolderInfo`/serialized `BackupItem`, exposed in client API types, and stored in nullable `BackupJob.scheduleFrequency` with migration/backfill. Manifest remains the recovery source of truth when DB history is missing.
- `[x]` Change `backup-database.ps1` so scheduled backups write to `BackupRoot\scheduled\<ScheduleFrequency>\<folderName>` while continuing to write `backupType: scheduled` and `scheduleFrequency` in `manifest.json`. The script rejects invalid frequencies through `ValidateSet`, keeps atomic `.inprogress` -> final rename behavior inside the selected bucket, and prints the final folder path for backend resolution.
- `[x]` Replace the two-part backup ID assumption with a safe relative-path ID. IDs encode the backup folder path relative to `BackupRoot`, support nested paths such as `scheduled/monthly/<folder>`, reject unsafe path parts, and continue to decode old two-part IDs during the transition.
- `[x]` Update backup root scanning to be bucket-aware. It scans canonical scheduled frequency buckets, legacy `scheduled/<folder>` folders, legacy top-level `daily/<folder>` scheduled backups, `manual`, and `pre-migration`. Support-package folders remain outside restorable inventory.
- `[x]` Update import registration for scheduled backups. Imported packages with `backupType: scheduled` and a valid `scheduleFrequency` land in the matching canonical bucket; legacy scheduled packages without `scheduleFrequency` fall back to the `daily` bucket without breaking restore safety.
- `[x]` Update scheduler due logic to compare the latest completed backup for the profile frequency being evaluated, not any scheduled backup. A due daily profile and a due monthly profile can both run as separate jobs without blocking each other.
- `[x]` Replace one-pool scheduled retention with per-frequency retention. Keep counts now cover `hourly`, `daily`, `weekly`, `monthly`, and `yearly`; legacy `retention.daily` remains backward-compatible; cleanup is limited to the completed backup's frequency bucket and avoids manual/pre-migration/support-package/unrelated frequency buckets.
- `[x]` Update restore safety for all supported layouts. `restore-production.ps1` now accepts canonical nested scheduled buckets and known legacy `daily` scheduled locations while still requiring the selected backup to be inside the configured `BackupRoot`, requiring `database.dump`, `manifest.json`, and `backup.log`, checking SHA-256/size, and refusing restore-test target databases.
- `[x]` Update Settings > Backups UI to display scheduled frequency separately from backup type and folder name. The table/details UI can show `Scheduled / Daily`, `Scheduled / Monthly`, etc., without relying on raw folder names.
- `[x]` Move per-frequency retention editing into Backup Automation schedule settings. The sidebar selects the automatic backup type and now contains each profile's on/off switch; the selected frequency's timing and keep-count controls appear inside `2. Schedule Settings`.
- `[x]` Convert Backup Automation from a single active schedule to multi-schedule profiles. The global automation toggle remains the master switch, and each frequency profile now has its own enabled/disabled state, timing, and keep count so customers can run combinations such as daily plus monthly while leaving hourly/weekly/yearly off.
- `[x]` Make short-month scheduling explicit. Monthly/yearly profiles accept day 1-31, and the backend clamps impossible dates such as day 31 in a 30-day month or February to that month's final day; the UI explains this directly below the day-of-month field.
- `[x]` Update `docs/backup-restore.md` after implementation to document the canonical folder layout, legacy compatibility rules, per-frequency retention policy, and manual recovery expectations for offline installations.

## Future Change Approval Flow

Every important change should follow this flow:

1. Record the goal in this checklist.
2. Identify the affected modules.
3. Run backend typecheck.
4. Run frontend typecheck if the client is affected.
5. Run lint or record why lint was not run.
6. Run the relevant automated tests.
7. If the change touches devices, run the relevant automated Device/K40 regressions; run the real-hardware checklist only when required by the owning phase/release gate and otherwise record it as `Not run`.
8. Record the test results.
9. Accept the change only after the relevant checks pass or risks are explicitly accepted.
